3644 B2BC C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3645 B2BE 0450  20 	b *r0
3646               	;
3647               	; 	' ------------------------------------------- ALPHA LOCK, CALIBRATED
3648               	; 	' On the TI, ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's VERTICAL axis.
3649               	; 	' Latched down it reports a direction that is NEVER RELEASED. Every other
3650               	; 	' game in this repo dodges this by not reading up/down at all; this one
3651               	; 	' cannot, because down is the duck and up is the elevator.
3652               	; 	'
3653               	; 	' THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUSED TO START UNTIL THE AXIS CLEARED, AND
3654               	; 	' THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. Classic99 defaults to invertcaps=1, so the TI
3655               	; 	' sees ALPHA LOCK DOWN when the host's Caps Lock is UP -- the normal
3656               	; 	' state. The game then sat on its title screen for ever waiting for a
3657               	; 	' condition the player had no reason to suspect, which presents as "the
3658               	; 	' title comes up and it will not start". A check that turns a survivable
3659               	; 	' input quirk into a dead game is worse than no check.
3660               	; 	'
3661               	; 	' So: sample the axis for 40 frames BEFORE any input can reasonably have
3662               	; 	' been given. A direction held for essentially all of them is not a player
3663               	; 	' -- it is the key. Record it, say so, and then IGNORE that direction for
3664               	; 	' the whole game, which makes ALPHA LOCK harmless instead of fatal.
3665               	; alock_cal:
3666               cvb_ALOCK_CAL

     B2C4 2175

3778               	; title_wait:
3779               cvb_TITLE_WAIT
3780               	; 	WAIT
3781 B3C8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B3CA A980     
3782 B3CC A656     	data wait
3783               	; 	' A KEY MUST BE HELD FOR THREE FRAMES TO COUNT.
3784               	; 	'
3785               	; 	' ALPHA LOCK shorts a keyboard line, and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps
3786               	; 	' so it reads DOWN with the host's Caps Lock UP -- the normal state of a
3787               	; 	' keyboard. cont1.key then returns values nobody pressed, a frame or two at
3788               	; 	' a time.
3789               	; 	'
3790               	; 	' That is fatal to the sequence below SPECIFICALLY BECAUSE its reset is
3791               	; 	' strict: anything that is not the next digit puts t838 back to 0, which is
3792               	; 	' what stops 8,5,3,8 opening the page. A spurious read BETWEEN the player's
3793               	; 	' own presses does exactly the same thing, so typing 8-3-8 cleared itself
3794               	; 	' halfway and appeared to do nothing -- and then typing 3-8 worked, because
3795               	; 	' the final 8 of the failed attempt was still standing as state. Reported
3796               	; 	' from play in precisely that shape.
3797               	; 	'
3798               	; 	' Three frames is 50 ms. A human press is several times that and the noise
3799               	; 	' is one or two, so the filter separates them cleanly. tkn caps AT the
3800               	; 	' threshold and the test is for equality, so a held key is accepted once
3801               	; 	' rather than on every frame it is down.
3802               	; 	tk = cont1.key
3803 B3CE D820  50 	movb @key1_data,@cvb_TK
     B3D0 8348     
     B3D2 2218     
3804               	; 	IF tk = tkr THEN
3805 B3D4 D020  34 	movb @cvb_TK,r0
     B3D6 2218

     B3DA 208F

     B3E0 B402

     B3E4 208D

3943               	; 	' FIRE ONLY, AND THAT IS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.
3944               	; 	'
3945               	; 	' `1` used to start the game as well, because on the TI joystick fire is
3946               	; 	' TAB -- neither guessable nor forgiving, since Windows treats a stray TAB
3947               	; 	' as a focus change and moves the whole window away. CLAUDE.md 3A still
3948               	; 	' says not to make FIRE the only way out of a title screen, and this screen
3949               	; 	' is the named exception.
3950               	; 	'
3951               	; 	' WHAT IT BUYS IS THE 838 SEQUENCE. Every digit on this screen now means
3952               	; 	' exactly one thing: a step of the cheat code, or a reset of it. While `1`
3953               	; 	' also meant START, mistyping one digit of 8-3-8 did not merely reset the
3954               	; 	' sequence -- it began a game, which is a far worse outcome than having to
3955               	; 	' type the code again, and it is the single most likely typo since 1 is
3956               	; 	' next to nothing on the sequence but adjacent to everything on a keypad.
3957               	; 	'
3958               	; 	' The hazard the digit guarded against is real but recoverable: a TAB that
3959               	; 	' moves focus leaves the title on screen and the player clicks back. A
3960               	; 	' game started by accident cannot be undone at all.
3961               	; 	IF cont1.button THEN RETURN
3962 B4E2 D020  30 	movb @joy1_data,r0
     B4E4 8346

     B4E8 4000

     B4FA B3C8     
3974               	;
3975               	; 	' 8-3-8 IS EDGE-TRIGGERED AND ANY STRAY DIGIT RESETS IT (see title_wait).
3976               	; 	'
3977               	; 	' It used to read cont1.key raw every pass and test only for the digit it
3978               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3979               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3980               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3981               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3982               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3983               	; 	'
3984               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3985               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3986               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3987               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3988               	; 	'
3989               	; 	' The reset is the DEFAULT rather than a test for a particular wrong digit:
3990               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.
3991               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------- 838 setup page

4366               	; 	' THE FIRE THAT STARTED THE ROUND IS NOT ALSO A JUMP. jrel is the jump's
4367               	; 	' release latch -- the button has to come UP between jumps -- and it was
4368               	; 	' never reset when play began, so it carried its value in from whatever
4369               	; 	' happened last. Press FIRE on the title with jrel left at 1 and Kelly
4370               	; 	' jumps on the first frame of the round, having been told to do so by the
4371               	; 	' keypress that only meant "start".
4372               	; 	'
4373               	; 	' It hid on the very first game of a session, because CVBasic zeroes its
4374               	; 	' variables and jrel = 0 already means "wait for a release" -- so it only
4375               	; 	' appeared on the SECOND round onwards, which reads as intermittent.
4376               	; 	'
4377               	; 	' start_krook is the one place worth doing this: new_game, losing a life
4378               	; 	' and advancing a Krook all pass through here, so every entry into play
4379               	; 	' demands a fresh press.

4387               	; 	' HARRY STARTS ON FLOOR 2 AT THE WEST EDGE OF SCREEN 7, and that is as
4388               	; 	' far left as the clock allows. The research says "the second-floor
4389               	; 	' elevator door", which is screen 3, and it cannot be done:
4390               	; 	'
4391               	; 	'   spawn      escape     round
4392               	; 	'   screen 3   119.9 s    100 s   -- impossible
4393               	; 	'   screen 5   108.1 s    100 s   -- impossible
4394               	; 	'   screen 6   102.3 s    100 s   -- impossible
4395               	; 	'   screen 7    96.4 s    100 s   -- 3.6 s of room
4396               	; 	'
4397               	; 	' HE STARTS AT THE LIFT NOW, AND THAT COST HIM A SPEED CHANGE.
4398               	; 	'
4399               	; 	' His escape route ZIGZAGS -- east along floor 2 to its escalator, WEST
4400               	; 	' along floor 3 to that one, then east again along the roof to the door.
4401               	; 	' From the lift on screen 3 that is 5,120 px against the 4,208 he walked
4402               	; 	' from screen 7, so at his old 1.75 px a pass he needed 105 s of a 100 s
4403               	; 	' round: he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4404               	; 	' would quietly have stopped existing. This block used to say exactly that
4405               	; 	' and conclude the lift was impossible. It was impossible AT THAT SPEED.
4406               	; 	'
4407               	; 	' AND QUARTER-PIXELS COULD NOT EXPRESS THE ANSWER. A quarter of a pixel
4408               	; 	' per pass is worth about TEN SECONDS of his route, so the two candidate
4409               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4410               	; 	'
4411               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4412               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4413               	; 	'
4414               	; 	' Worse, 2.25 was not reachable at all: the accumulator below had two
4415               	; 	' drain steps and therefore a hard ceiling of 2 px a pass, so hsp4 = 9
4416               	; 	' silently delivered 2.00 and he fell two thirds of a screen short of his
4417               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4418               	; 	'
4419               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,
4420               	; 	' and hsp64 = 35 (2.1875 px/pass) lands him at 96.3 s of the 100 --
4421               	; 	' escaping as the timer expires, which is what was asked for.
4422               	; 	'
4423               	; 	' 35 AND NOT 34, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE PLAYER'S OWN SCREEN. Measured
4424               	; 	' in play at 34: standing on an ordinary screen he escaped with one timer
4425               	; 	' unit left, and standing on the ELEVATOR screen he ran out of clock --
4426               	; 	' same route, same constant, decided by where the player happened to be.
4427               	; 	' Everything that moves is paced per loop PASS while the clock is paced in
4428               	; 	' real FRAMES, so a busy screen slows Harry in real time and the clock does
4429               	; 	' not slow with him. A whole round measured 2,335 passes over about 98 s --
4430               	; 	' 23.8 a second, not the 25 the model assumes -- and the heavy screens are
4431               	; 	' slower again. 35 buys the margin that covers the worst of them. The
4432               	; 	' underlying split is recorded in DESIGN.md 0f-ter; this constant only
4433               	; 	' papers over it.
4434               	; 	'
4435               	; 	' The margin
4436               	; 	' that remains is the ROUND's, not the chase's: Kelly still has to get
4437               	; 	' there first, and does. assets/checkchase.py walks both routes out of
4438               	; 	' these constants, models the drain ceiling, and fails the build if the
4439               	; 	' configured speed is one the accumulator cannot deliver.

4605               	;
4606               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4607               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4608               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4609               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4610               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4611               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4612               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4613               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4614               	; 	'
4615               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4616               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4617               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4618               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4619               	; 	'
4620               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4621               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4622               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4623               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4624               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4625               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4626               	; 	' anybody takes.
4627               	; 	'
4628               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4629               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4630               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4631               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.

4657 B95E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4658 B960 0450  20 	b *r0
4659               	;
4660               	; 	' ======================================================================
4661               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4662               	; 	' ======================================================================
4663               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4664               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4665               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4666               	; 	' of running.
4667               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4668               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4669               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4670               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4671               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4672               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4673               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4674               	; 	'
4675               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4676               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4677               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4678               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4679               	; 	'
4680               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4681               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4682               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4683               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4684               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4685               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4686               	; 	'
4687               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4688               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4689               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4690               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4691               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4692               	; 	'
4693               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4694               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4695               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4696               	; draw_screen:
4697               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4698               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4699 B962 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B964 A980     
4700 B966 EB42     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4701               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4702 B968 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B96A A980     
4703 B96C BD56     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4704               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4705 B96E 04C0  14 	clr r0
4706 B970 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B972 21B7     
4707               cv136
4708               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4709               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4710               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4711               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4712               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4713               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4714               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4715               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4716               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4717               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.
4718               	; 		WAIT
4719 B974 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B976 A980

4806               	;
4807               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4808               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4809               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4810               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4811               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4812               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4813               	; 	'
4814               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4815               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4816               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4817               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4818               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4819               	; 	'
4820               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4821               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4822               	; 	' above it at all.
4823               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4824               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4825               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4826               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4827               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4828               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4829               	; 	'
4830               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4831               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4832               	; beam_one:
4833               cvb_BEAM_ONE

5478               	; 			' A RADIO NEEDS A CELL, NOT A PIXEL, because it is drawn as
5479               	; 			' characters. Divided by repeated subtraction: `/` compiles
5480               	; 			' to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A), and this runs once
5481               	; 			' per screen where the divide would run per obstacle -- so
5482               	; 			' the loop is the cheaper of the two, and the only one that
5483               	; 			' cannot be got wrong by a rounding rule.

5490               	; 			' PER KIND, not one global speed (see start_krook).
5491               	; 			' THE SPEED IS A PROPERTY OF THE KIND, and obs() only ever
5492               	; 			' held a copy of it -- set from the kind here, zeroed for a
5493               	; 			' radio below, and read in one place. upd_obst derives it
5494               	; 			' from obk() instead, which is 8 bytes of RAM back and one
5495               	; 			' fewer thing that can disagree with itself.
5496               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5497               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5498               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5499               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5500               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5501               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5502               	; 			'
5503               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5504               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5505               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5506               	; 			'
5507               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5508               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5509               	; 			'
5510               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5511               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5512               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5513               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5514               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5515               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5516               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5517               	; 			' reported from play.
5518               	; 			'
5519               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5520               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5521               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5522               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5523               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5524               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5525               	; 			'
5526               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5527               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5528               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5529               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5530               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5531               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5532               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5533               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5534               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5535               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5536               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5537               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5538               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5539               	; 			stag = lx AND 63

5620               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5621               	; 			'
5622               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5623               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5624               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5625               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5626               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5627               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5628               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5629               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5630               	; 			'
5631               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5632               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5633               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5634               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5635               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5636               	; 			'
5637               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5638               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5639               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5640               	; 			'
5641               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5642               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5643               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5644               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5645               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5646               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5647               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5648 BFFE D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0

6296               	;
6297               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row
6298               	; 	' (crw 0-3) and the door state (cst). Four columns and four rows, so
6299               	; 	' sixteen cells, and the three interesting things about a cell are all
6300               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
6301               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
6302               	; 	'
6303               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
6304               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
6305               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
6306               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge
6307               	; 	' with nothing behind it.
6308               	; 	'
6309               	; 	' Nested IFs, never `ccl > 0 AND ccl < 3` -- the 9900 backend miscompiles
6310               	; 	' a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).

6319               	; 	' is then continuous across all four columns of the opening on every
6320               	; 	' floor, including the three the car is not on. It spans the opening and
6321               	; 	' not the jambs: EJAMBL/EJAMBR sit in the wall column either side and

6535               cv303
6536               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6537               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6538               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6539               	; 		'
6540               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6541               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6542               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6543               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6544               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6545               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6546               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6547               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6548               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6549               	; 		'
6550               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6551               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6552               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6553               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6554               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6555               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
6556 C73A D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6861               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6862               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6863               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6864               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6865               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6866               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6867               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6868               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was

7079               cv386
7080               	; 		' THE FIRST STEP OFF THE MARK IS ALWAYS HEARD. sfw is a distance --
7081               	; 		' a step every fifteen pixels -- so a short tap of the stick moved
7082               	; 		' him a few pixels and made NO sound at all. That does not read as
7083               	; 		' a short step, it reads as the controls being ignored, and it is
7084               	; 		' worst exactly where a player taps rather than holds: lining up a
7085               	; 		' jump, or edging around a hazard.
7086               	; 		'
7087               	; 		' Priming the accumulator to the threshold makes the very next pass
7088               	; 		' fire. It does not cheat the RATE: the trigger subtracts fifteen
7089               	; 		' rather than zeroing, so the pixels actually travelled still carry
7090               	; 		' into the following step and a held run comes out at 6.9 a second
7091               	; 		' either way.
7092               	; 		IF kmvl = 0 THEN sfw = 15
7093 CB4A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KMVL,r0
     CB4C 2209     
7094 CB4E 1302  14 	jeq cv388
7095 CB50 0460  28 	b @cv387
     CB52 CB5C     
7096               cv388
7097 CB54 0200  20 	li r0,3840   ; 15*256
     CB56 0F00     
7098 CB58 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SFW
     CB5A 220C     
7099               cv387
7100               	; 		' AN ODOMETER, NOT A CLOCK -- pixels travelled, exactly like
7101               	; 		' Harry's hanim. It counted PASSES once, which made his legs cycle
7102               	; 		' slower on the escalator screens while he still ran at the same
7103               	; 		' speed; that was fixed by counting the frame DELTA instead, which
7104               	; 		' traded one mismatch for another -- his legs then kept wall-clock
7105               	; 		' time while his body still moved once per pass, so his stride
7106               	; 		' slipped against the ground whenever the loop was busy.
7107               	; 		'
7108               	; 		' Distance settles it. A pose per 8 px at any frame rate, and a
7109               	; 		' footstep is a stride rather than a tick.
7110               	; 		kanim = kanim + kspd

     CB5E 2101

7226               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7227               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7228               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7229               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7230               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7231               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7232               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7233               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7234               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7235               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7236               	; 	'
7237               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7238               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7239               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7240               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
7241               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7800               	;
7801               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7802               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7803               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7804               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7805               	; 	'
7806               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7807               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7808               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7809               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7810               	; 	'
7811               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7812               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7813               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7814               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7815               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7816               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7817               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7818               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7819               	; 	'
7820               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7821               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7822               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7823               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7824               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7825               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7826               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7827               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7828               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7829               	; 	'
7830               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7831               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7832               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7833               	; wipe_shelf:
7834               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7835               	; 	wsn = 8

7906               	;
7907               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7908               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7909               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7910               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7911               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7912               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7913               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7914               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7915               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7916               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7917               	; 	' nothing.
7918               	; 	'
7919               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7920               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7921               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7922               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no
7923               	; 	' fixture sits close enough for the two-cell clear at #bca and #bca+1 to

7978               	;
7979               	; 	' THE SAME DISTINCTION ONE ROW LOWER. A radio or a prize stands in the
7980               	; 	' bottom half of a band, so the two cells above it have to be cleared or
7981               	; 	' the pillar it covers keeps its top half hanging in the air. That clear
7982               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the
7983               	; 	' cell really is a pillar: at column 0 and 31 it is the OUTSIDE WALL, and
7984               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
7985               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
7986               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round
7987               	; 	' start: the wall went up and was taken straight back down again.
7988               	; 	'
7989               	; 	' So it tests for the thing it is allowed to remove rather than trusting
7990               	; 	' the position. COUNTR is a pillar or a counter; anything else stays.
7991               	; wall_clear:

8473               	; upd_obst:
8474               cvb_UPD_OBST
8475               	; 	' ONE ACCUMULATOR PER KIND, NOT PER SLOT. Every hazard of a kind moves at
8476               	; 	' that kind's speed, so three calls a pass serve all eight slots.
8477               	; 	'
8478               	; 	' Per-slot would have meant up to eight calls of O(fdv) work every pass --
8479               	; 	' the positive feedback loop CLAUDE.md 3A warns about, where a slow pass is
8480               	; 	' made slower by the very code meant to take the loop rate out of the
8481               	; 	' game's behaviour. It also saves the eight bytes an obacc() would cost,
8482               	; 	' and ColecoVision RAM is the tighter budget.
8483               	; 	pacc = oacb

     D598 20F7     
8516               	; 	' THE BOUNCE PHASE, ON THE SAME CLOCK AS THE JUMP AND AT ITS OLD PERIOD.
8517               	; 	'
8518               	; 	' The jump has always advanced by the frame delta (`kjf = kjf + fdv`) while
8519               	; 	' the bounce advanced once per PASS -- so whether a jump cleared a ball
8520               	; 	' depended on how busy the screen was, and the frame-by-frame property
8521               	; 	' checkball.py proves was exact only at fdv = 1.
8522               	; 	'
8523               	; 	' Stepping the phase by fdv directly would fix the clock and wreck the
8524               	; 	' game: a 32-phase arc would drop from about 1.35 s to 0.55 s, balls
8525               	; 	' bouncing 2.4x faster everywhere. So it gets a fraction of its own --
8526               	; 	' 25/64 of a phase per frame is 23.4 phases a second, against the 23.8 it
8527               	; 	' runs at today. Same bounce, now the same clock as the thing that has to
8528               	; 	' clear it.
8529               	; 	pacc = obac

8744 D776 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8745 D778 0450  20 	b *r0
8746               	;
8747               	; 	' ONE STEP OF A FRACTIONAL SPEED, FOR WHOEVER IS MOVING.
8748               	; 	'
8749               	; 	' `pacc` in and out, `psp64` in (sixty-fourths of a pixel per FRAME),
8750               	; 	' `pspd` out (whole pixels this pass). CVBasic has no locals, so the caller
8751               	; 	' stages its own accumulator in and takes it back out; that costs four
8752               	; 	' statements against the fourteen this used to be, written out per actor.
8753               	; 	'
8754               	; 	' Harry had the only copy. Kelly, the hazards and the bounce phase all need
8755               	; 	' the same thing -- everything the round clock judges has to advance by
8756               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAMES, or the loop rate becomes a difficulty dial and where the
8757               	; 	' player stands decides the outcome (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8758               	; 	'
8759               	; 	' TWO DRAINS CAP THE STEP AT 2 PER FRAME whatever psp64 says (CLAUDE.md
8760               	; 	' 3A: N drains cap the speed at N, and the surplus leaks into a byte that
8761               	; 	' wraps). Every caller is under that: Kelly 102/64 = 1.6, the fastest
8762               	; 	' hazard 76/64 = 1.2, Harry 57/64 = 0.9, the bounce phase 25/64 = 0.4.
8763               	; 	'
8764               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. At 2 px a frame five frames is 10 px,
8765               	; 	' and to skip a position window an actor must start outside it on one side
8766               	; 	' and land outside on the other, which needs 12. Three was the original bug
8767               	; 	' in miniature: the lift screen runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second,
8768               	; 	' which IS fdv = 3, so every hitch there lost a frame. The clamp exists for
8769               	; 	' a genuine stall and at five it only fires for one.
8770               	; pace_step:
8771               cvb_PACE_STEP
8772               	; 	pfd = fdv
8773 D77A D820  54 	movb @cvb_FDV,@cvb_PFD
     D77C 212E     
     D77E 215E     
8774               	; 	IF pfd > 5 THEN pfd = 5

     D782 215E

     D786 0600

     D78E D798

9256               	;
9257               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
9258               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
9259               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
9260               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
9261               	; 	' runs every pass.
9262               	; 	'
9263               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
9264               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
9265               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
9266               	; 	'
9267               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
9268               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
9269               	; 	'
9270               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
9271               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
9272               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
9273               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
9274               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
9275               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
9276               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
9277               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
9278               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
9279               	; 	'
9280               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
9281               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
9282               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
9283               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
9284               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
9285               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
9286               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
9287               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
9288               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
9289               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
9290               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
9291               	; 	' flight looking busy.
9292               	; 	'
9293               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
9294               	; 	'
9295               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
9296               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
9297               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
9298               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
9299               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
9300               	; 	'
9301               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
9302               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
9303               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
9304               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
9305               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN

9386               cv598
9387               	;
9388               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9389               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9390               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9391               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9392               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9393               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9394               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9395               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9396               	; 	'
9397               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9398               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9399               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9400               	; 	'
9401               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9402               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9403               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9404               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9405               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9406               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9407               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9408               	; 	'
9409               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9410               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9411               	; 	'
9412               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9413               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9414               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9415               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9416               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9417               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9418               	; 	'
9419               	; 	' The clamp and the drains live in pace_step now, with the reasoning; the
9420               	; 	' numbers above are what sized them. Measured: TIME 02 on the lift screen
9421               	; 	' against TIME 04 on a light one when the clamp was 3, and 00 against 09
9422               	; 	' before any of this.
9423               	; 	'
9424               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9425               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9426               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9427               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9428               	; 	' journey.
9429               	; 	'
9430               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9431               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9432               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.

9596               	; 	' THE CYCLE ADVANCES BY PIXELS TRAVELLED, NOT BY TIME. This was `+ fdv`,
9597               	; 	' which is a clock -- and Harry's speed is a QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR, so
9598               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took
9599               	; 	' another step while the ground did not move, which is precisely what
9600               	; 	' scrubbing your feet looks like. It is the classic foot-slip: the legs
9601               	; 	' and the world running off two different clocks.
9602               	; 	'
9603               	; 	' Advancing by hspd locks the stride to the floor -- he cannot take a step
9604               	; 	' without covering ground, or cover ground without taking one, at any

9863 DF40 0450  20 	b *r0
9864               	;
9865               	; 	' ======================================================================
9866               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9867               	; 	' ======================================================================
9868               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9869               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9870               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9871               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9872               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9873               	; 	'
9874               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9875               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9876               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9877               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9878               	; 	' broken.
9879               	; 	'
9880               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9881               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9882               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9883               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9884               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9885               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9886               	; 	'
9887               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9888               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9889               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9890               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9891               	; coll_obst:
9892               cvb_COLL_OBST
9893               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9894 DF42 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DF44 21B2     
9895 DF46 0201  20 	li r1,768   ; 3*256
     DF48 0300

9898 DF4E 0460  28 	b @cv676
     DF50 DF56

10190               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
10191               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
10192               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
10193               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
10194               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
10195               	; 	' away from.
10196               	; 	'
10197               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
10198               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
10199               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
10200               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
10201               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
10202               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
10203               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
10204               	; 	'
10205               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
10206               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
10207               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
10208               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.

10422               	; draw_actors:

10424               	; 	' RIDING WITH THE DOORS SHUT: HIDE HIM AND DO NOT DRAW HIM AT ALL.
10425               	; 	'
10426               	; 	' This test used to sit AFTER his sprites had been written, so every pass
10427               	; 	' placed him at his old floor and then hid him again. That is not free.
10428               	; 	' CVBasic's SPRITE writes a RAM mirror which the vblank ISR copies to
10429               	; 	' VRAM, so a vblank landing BETWEEN the draw and the hide latches the
10430               	; 	' visible state for a frame -- and draw_actors is long (Kelly, Harry,
10431               	; 	' eight obstacles) while a pass on the lift screen spans two or three
10432               	; 	' frames, so it happened constantly. He flickered at the floor he had
10433               	; 	' just left, which reads as a drawing bug rather than as a journey.
10434               	; 	'
10435               	; 	' Deciding BEFORE drawing costs one test and cannot race the ISR at all:
10436               	; 	' the mirror never holds a position to be caught with.
10437               	; 	IF klst = ST_ELEV THEN
10438 E362 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E364 21B2

10514               cv739
10515               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10516               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10517               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10518               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10519               	; 	'
10520               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10521               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10522               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10523               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10524               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10525               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10526               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
10527               	; 	ky = flry(klv)
10528 E400 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLV,r0
     E402 209B

10671               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10672               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10673               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10674               	; 		'
10675               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10676               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10677               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10678               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10679               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
10680               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
10681 E544 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E546 3800     
10682 E548 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KQ
     E54A 21DC     
10683               	; 		' BITS 3 AND 4, NOT 2 AND 3 -- A POSE EVERY 8 PIXELS, which is
10684               	; 		' Harry's beat (hanim AND 8 / AND 16) and now means the same thing,
10685               	; 		' because both counters hold pixels travelled. On the old 4-count
10686               	; 		' beat an odometer would have flipped his legs every single pass at
10687               	; 		' 4 px a pass, which is a blur rather than a run. checkanim reads
10688               	; 		' these bits out of the source, and fails if two bands of one figure
10689               	; 		' run on different ones -- so the body below moves with them.
10690               	; 		IF kanim AND 8 THEN kq = kq + 4

10875               	; 	' `hanim` advances by `hspd`, which is PIXELS MOVED THIS PASS, so a pose
10876               	; 	' is a DISTANCE and not a duration -- it cannot drift with the frame rate
10877               	; 	' or scrub his feet at any speed.
10878               	; 	'
10879               	; 	' HALVING THIS WAS TRIED AND IS WORSE. Bits 4 and 5 put a pose on 16 px
10880               	; 	' and the cycle on 64, which is closer to a real stride for a figure 24 px

     E73C 21C4     
10918               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10919               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10920               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10921               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10922               	; 	'
10923               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10924               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10925               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10926               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10927               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10928               	; 	'
10929               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10930               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10931               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10932               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10933               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10934               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10935               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10936               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10977               	; 	' half a stride behind his shirt. Same reason hqs comes from hq.

11011               cv774
11012               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
11013               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
11014               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
11015               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
11016               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
11017               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
11018               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
11019               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
11020               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
11021               	; 	'
11022               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
11023               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
11024               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
11025               	; 	' a jump.
11026               	; 	'
11027               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
11028               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
11029               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
11030               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
11031               	; 	'
11032               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
11033               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
11034               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
11035               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
11036               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
11037               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
11038               	; 	'
11039               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
11040               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
11041               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
11042               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
11043               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
11044               	; 	'
11045               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
11046               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
11047               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
11048               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
11049               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.
11050               	; 	IF hanim AND 8 THEN hy = hy - 1

11517 EBC6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11518 EBC8 0450  20 	b *r0
11519               	;
11520               	; 	' ======================================================================
11521               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11522               	; 	' ======================================================================
11523               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11524               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11525               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11526               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11527               	; 	'
11528               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11529               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11530               	; 	'
11531               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11532               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11533               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11534               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11535               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11536               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11537               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11538               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11539               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11540               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11541               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11542               	; esc_tick:
11543               cvb_ESC_TICK
11544               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11545               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11546               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11547               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11548               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11549 EBCA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EBCC 219E     
11550 EBCE 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EBD0 0100     
11551 EBD2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11552 EBD4 1402  14 	jhe cv813

     EBDC 219E

     EBE0 0700

11566               cv812
11567               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11568               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11569               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11570               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11571               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11572               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11573               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11574               	; 	'
11575               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11576               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11577               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11578               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11579               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11580               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11581               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11582               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11583 EBEE D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EBF0 20B3     
11584 EBF2 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EBF4 0100

     EBF8 20B3

11595               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11596               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11597               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11598               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11599               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11600               	; 	'
11601               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11602               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11603               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11604               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11605               	; 	'
11606               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11607               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11608               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11609               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11610               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11611               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11612               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11613               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11614               	; 	'
11615               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11616               	; 	' move at all.

11877 EE4A 0450  20 	b *r0
11878               	;
11879               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11880               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11881               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11882               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11883               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11884               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11885               	; 	'
11886               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11887               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11888               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11889               	; 	'
11890               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11891               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11892               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11893               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11894               	; 	'
11895               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11896               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11897               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11898               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11899               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11900               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11901               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11902               	; 	'
11903               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11904               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11905               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11906               	; 	'
11907               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11908               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11909               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11910               	; scan_furn:
11911               cvb_SCAN_FURN
11912               	; 	FOR fl = 0 TO 3
11913 EE4C 04C0  14 	clr r0
11914 EE4E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_FL
     EE50 21BE     
11915               cv853
11916               	; 		GOSUB scan_base
11917 EE52 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     EE54 A980     
11918 EE56 EFE8     	data cvb_SCAN_BASE

11920               	; 		say = fbase + 3
11921 EE58 D020  34 	movb @cvb_FBASE,r0

11952               	; 		' THE ESCALATOR DIAGONALS USED TO BE DRAWN HERE TOO, and it was dead
11953               	; 		' work: scan_escs runs immediately below and draws the same three rows
11954               	; 		' for the same three floors. Every input is a pure function of fl --
11955               	; 		' scan_base, the side lookup, the masks, scan_pat -- and scan_or1 is an
11956               	; 		' OR, so doing it twice cannot differ from doing it once. Sixteen
11957               	; 		' statements, about 320 bytes, and nine VDP writes a floor that nothing
11958               	; 		' on screen could reflect.
11959               	; 		'
11960               	; 		' It is the shape a routine takes when a feature is split out and the
11961               	; 		' original is left behind -- scan_escs is this block plus the colour
11962               	; 		' write that was added later, and it carried a copy of the explanatory
11963               	; 		' comment as well as the code.
11964               	; 		'
11965               	; 		' A SCREENSHOT COULD NOT HAVE SETTLED IT: the radar is three pixel rows
11966               	; 		' tall and Classic99 clips the bottom of the screen. It was proved by
11967               	; 		' replaying both versions against a model of the pattern table for all

12249 F0DE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
12250 F0E0 0450  20 	b *r0
12251               	;
12252               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12253               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12254               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12255               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12256               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12257               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12258               	; scan_wipe:
12259               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12260               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12261               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12262               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12263               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12264               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12265               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12266               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12267               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12268               	; 	'
12269               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12270               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12271               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12272               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12273               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12274               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12275               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12276               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12277               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12278               	; 	'
12279               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12280               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12281               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12282               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12283               	; 	'
12284               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12285               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
