12354               	;
12355               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12356               	; 	'
12357               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12358               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12359               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12360               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12361               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12362               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12363               	; 	'
12364               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12365               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12366               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12367               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12368               	; 	'
12369               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12370               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12371               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12372               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12373               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12374               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12375               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12376               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12377               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12378               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12379               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
12380               	; 	say = 3 - elvl

13004               	; 		' BOTH THE LABEL AND THE NUMBER. Blinking the word alone left the
13005               	; 		' digits sitting there steady, so the thing that was actually

13169               	;
13170               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
13171               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
13172               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
13173               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
13174               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
13175               	; 	'
13176               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
13177               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
13178               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
13179               	; 	'
13180               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
13181               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests

13183               	; 	'
13184               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
13185               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

13289               	;
13290               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13291               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13292               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13293               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13294               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13295               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13296               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13297               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13298               	; 	'
13299               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13300               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13301               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13302               	;
13303               	; do_escape:
13304               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
13305               	; 	escapd = 0

13342 F8B8 EB8C     	data cvb_HIDE_ALL
13343               	; 	' THE MESSAGE BOX, ON THE GAME SCREEN AND NOT INSTEAD OF IT.
13344               	; 	'
13345               	; 	' It used to CLS before GAME OVER, so the last thing the player saw was
13346               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13347               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store
13348               	; 	' stays; the message sits in the middle of it.
13349               	; 	'
13350               	; 	' THE BOX IS THE FONT'S OWN BACKGROUND. Every font character is black on
13351               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13352               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and
13353               	; 	' no new characters, and it reads against the store because nothing else on
13354               	; 	' the playfield is that colour.
13355               	; 	'
13356               	; 	' THIRTEEN WIDE, WHICH IS THE LONGEST MESSAGE PLUS ONE EITHER SIDE.
13357               	; 	' HE GOT AWAY and THE BIPLANE are both eleven. Every string below is padded
13358               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it
13359               	; 	' at columns 10-22 -- centre 16, the screen's.
13360               	; 	'
13361               	; 	' THE REASON IS ALWAYS ON THE SAME ROW, and that is what lets one layout
13362               	; 	' serve both cases. Losing a life draws three rows around it; losing the
13363               	; 	' last one draws two more ON TOP, so GAME OVER appears above the reason
13364               	; 	' rather than replacing it. Printing the reason at a different row for each
13365               	; 	' case would need `PRINT AT` with a variable, and every other PRINT in this
13366               	; 	' program uses a constant.
13367               	; 	PRINT AT 330,"             "
13368 F8BA 0200  20 	li r0,330
     F8BC 014A     
13369 F8BE C800  30 	mov r0,@cursor
     F8C0 8342     
13370 F8C2 0202  20 	li r2,cv947
     F8C4 F8D4     
13371 F8C6 0203  20 	li r3,13
     F8C8 000D     
13372 F8CA 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     F8CC A980     
13373 F8CE A11C     	data print_string
13374 F8D0 0460  28 	b @cv948
     F8D2 F8E2     
13375               cv947

     F8D6 2020     
     F8D8 2020

13530               	;
13531               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13532               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13533               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13534               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13535               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13536               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13537               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13538               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13539               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13540               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13541               	; 	' Harry was caught.
13542               	; snd_off:

13638               	; 	' ======================================================================
13639               	; 	' SOUND
13640               	; 	' ======================================================================
13641               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
13642               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
13643               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
13644               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
13645               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

13689               cv975
13690               	; 	' A PRIZE IS AN ARPEGGIO -- testsounds PICKUP C. C5, E5, G5, then a fade
13691               	; 	' on the top note: it RISES, which is what makes it read as a reward
13692               	; 	' rather than as an event, and it ARRIVES, because a major triad resolves
13693               	; 	' where an arbitrary rise just stops.
13694               	; 	'
13695               	; 	' It was two notes struck together on two channels and held. That needed
13696               	; 	' both channels for one sound, said nothing in particular, and had no
13697               	; 	' shape at all -- a chord is a texture, and this wants to be a phrase.
13698               	; 	'
13699               	; 	' NOT THE MEASURED ONE, ON PURPOSE. The 2600 tops its pickup out at
13700               	; 	' 2543 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek; TIA's softer timbre
13701               	; 	' carries it and the SN76489's does not. This peaks at 784 Hz. Copying the
13702               	; 	' recording would have been faithful and unpleasant, and the player earns
13703               	; 	' this sound dozens of times a round.
13704               	; 	IF sfp = 1 THEN
13705 FB68 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFP,r0
     FB6A 2208     
13706 FB6C 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     FB6E 0100

13721               	; 	' volume are chosen from the count rather than stored in a table, which
13722               	; 	' for six phases is smaller than the table would be.

13874               	;
13875               	; 	' the jump sweep: divisor falling = pitch rising
13876               	; 	' THE JUMP IS A WARBLE, NOT A SWEEP -- testsounds variant A, measured off
13877               	; 	' the 2600 at about 415 Hz alternating with 188 for roughly 280 ms.
13878               	; 	'
13879               	; 	' It used to be a rising sweep, 500 down to 120 in steps of 40. That was
13880               	; 	' invented rather than measured, and it had the wrong SHAPE: a sweep reads
13881               	; 	' as something departing, which is a fine idea and is not what the original
13882               	; 	' does. Eight ticks alternating is four cycles of warble, which is what the
13883               	; 	' recording shows.
13884               	; 	'
13885               	; 	' 270 is 415 Hz and 595 is 188 Hz (divisor = 3579545 / (32 * f), so a
13886               	; 	' SMALLER divisor is a HIGHER note -- the pair reads backwards from how it
13887               	; 	' is written).
13888               	; 	IF swt > 0 THEN
13889 FCEE D020  34 	movb @cvb_SWT,r0
     FCF0 2096

13944               cv999
13945               	;
13946               	; 	' THE LOW-TIME WARNING IS SILENT. There was a beep a second under ten, and
13947               	; 	' it was the wrong instrument for the job: the last ten seconds are the
13948               	; 	' busiest part of a round, so a repeating tone arrives exactly when the
13949               	; 	' player most needs to hear the hazards, and it fought the prize arpeggio
13950               	; 	' for channel 2 as well. The flash says the same thing and says it in the
13951               	; 	' place the player is already looking.
13952               	;
13953               	; 	' FOOTSTEPS ARE NOISE, AND ON THE NOISE CHANNEL -- testsounds variant A.
13954               	; 	'
13955               	; 	' They were two alternating TONES on channel 0, which is neither what the
13956               	; 	' original does nor a good use of a channel: the recording says a footstep
13957               	; 	' is a short burst of white noise, and channel 3 was sitting unused while
13958               	; 	' the footsteps and the jump sweep shared channel 0 and had to take turns.
13959               	; 	'
13960               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS RUN D: a SHORT tick at the measured RATE.
13961               	; 	'
13962               	; 	' The two dials are independent and both were tried at the bench. The
13963               	; 	' 2600 plays a 64 ms burst every 165 ms -- six a second -- and variant A
13964               	; 	' copies both. Ten a second was tried on the way here and reads as
13965               	; 	' hurrying rather than running. What D changes is not the cadence but the
13966               	; 	' TICK: two frames rather than four, and a notch louder to carry at half
13967               	; 	' the length. Same six a second, less of it.
13968               	; 	'
13969               	; 	' EIGHT A SECOND, AND THE ACCUMULATOR NOW CARRIES ITS REMAINDER.
13970               	; 	'
13971               	; 	' sfw counted pixels and the trigger did `sfw = 0`, THROWING AWAY whatever
13972               	; 	' was past the threshold. kspd is about four pixels a pass, so a threshold
13973               	; 	' of 17 did not fire at 17 -- it fired at the first multiple past it, about
13974               	; 	' 20, and every rate this was ever set to came out slower than the number
13975               	; 	' said: the "six a second" was 5.2 and the "ten" that read as hurrying was
13976               	; 	' really 8.7. Subtracting the threshold instead keeps the fraction, so the
13977               	; 	' rate is what it claims to be at any walking speed.
13978               	; 	'
13979               	; 	' 104 px/s over 15 px is 6.9 a second -- between the 8.0 that read as
13980               	; 	' slightly hurried and the 6.1 that read as trudging. The dial is this one
13981               	; 	' constant and the subtraction below, which must stay threshold + 1 or the
13982               	; 	' carry is wrong.
13983               	; 	'
13984               	; 	' SOT = 2 IS THE SHORTEST TICK THERE IS, and 1 is SILENCE. The decay block
13985               	; 	' below runs LATER IN THE SAME sfx_tick, so a count of 1 is decremented to
13986               	; 	' zero on the pass that set it and the note-off fires before the sound has
13987               	; 	' lasted any time at all. 2 survives to the next pass, which is one pass of
13988               	; 	' sound -- about 42 ms against the bench's 33, as close as a per-pass timer
13989               	; 	' gets, and the reason the bench and the game cannot sound identical.
13990               	; 	'
13991               	; 	' Register 4 is white noise at the fastest rate.
13992               	; 	'
13993               	; 	' VOLUME 9, WHICH IS UNDER EVERYTHING ELSE ON PURPOSE. The hit, the prize
13994               	; 	' and the jump are 12 and 13, and they happen ONCE; footsteps play for the
13995               	; 	' whole round. At 13 they were level with the events they are supposed to
13996               	; 	' sit beneath, so the effects that carry information had nothing to stand
13997               	; 	' out from. The tone version was 7 for the same reason -- noise does read
13998               	; 	' quieter than a tone at the same number, which is why this is 9 and not
13999               	; 	' back to 7.
14000               	; 	IF sfw > 14 THEN

14120               	; 	RETURN
14121 FE78 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
14122 FE7A 0450  20 	b *r0
14123               	;
14124               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
14125               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
14126               	; 	'
14127               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
14128               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
14129               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
14130               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
14131               	; 	'
14132               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
14133               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
14134               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
14135               	; 	'
14136               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
14137               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
14138               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
14139               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
14140               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
14141               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
14142               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
14143               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
14144               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
14145               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
14146               	; 	#if TI994A
14147               	; 	BANK 1
14148      0182     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

14156               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14157               	; 	'
14158               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
14159               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
14160               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
14161               	; 	'
14162               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
14163               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
14164               	; 	' letter after it.
14165               	;

14286               	;
14287               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).
14288               	; 	'
14289               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col, which is ONE synchronous call that
14290               	; 	' writes all three screen thirds (define_color always does the LDIRVM3 triple
14291               	; 	' copy). It replaced a VPOKE loop that wrote this one value 1,416 times paced by
14292               	; 	' 24 WAITs -- the largest scripted delay in the boot, and the reason the title
14293               	; 	' screen used to fill in visibly rather than appearing at once.
14294               	; 	'
14295               	; 	' EIGHT BYTES PER CHARACTER, not one. Supply fewer and DEFINE COLOR reads
14296               	; 	' whatever follows in ROM as colour data, with no error at build or run time.
14297               	;
14298               	; font_col:
14299               cvb_FONT_COL

14418               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14419               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14420               	; 	'
14421               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14422               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14423               	; 	' being small sprites.
14424               	; 	'
14425               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14426               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14427               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14428               	; 	'
14429               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14430               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14431               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14432               	;
14433               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14434               cvb_SPR_KELLY

15431               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
15432               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
15433               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
15434               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15435               	;
15436               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15437               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15438               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15439               	; 	' unreadable.
15440               	;
15441               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

15551               	; 	'   179 SCANBK
15552               	; 	'   180 SLABP
15553               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15554               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15555               	; 	'
15556               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15557               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15558               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15559               	; 	'
15560               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15561               	; 	' blit is
15562               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15563               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15564               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15565               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15566               	; 	'
15567               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15568               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15569               	; 	'
15570               	; 	' template ids:
15571               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15572               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15573               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
